> AGENT

All clean. Here's the agent identity resolution:

**Priority for `agent` field on hash-derived sessions:**
1. `X-Brain-Agent-Type` header (explicit, e.g. `"coding-agent"`, `"architect"`)
2. `User-Agent` containing `"claude-cli"` → `"claude-code"`
3. Fallback → `"proxy"` (unknown caller)

> DEVELOPER

claude-code -> claude-cli

     1→# ADR-050: Conversation Hash Correlation via UUIDv5
     2→
     3→## Status
     4→
     5→Proposed
     6→
     7→## Context
     8→
     9→The LLM proxy needs to correlate traces into conversations for Observer analysis and cost attribution. LLM API requests (Anthropic Messages API, OpenAI Chat Completions) contain the full conversation history -- each request is a superset of the previous. The system prompt + first user message is stable across all requests in a conversation.
    10→
    11→Session IDs (from CLI/orchestrator) are not always available -- unknown clients have no session. The proxy needs a session-independent correlation mechanism that works for all clients.
    12→
    13→The `conversation` table already exists in the schema. The codebase uses UUIDs everywhere -- web UI conversations use UUIDv4.
    14→
    15→## Decision
    16→
    17→The proxy derives a deterministic conversation ID using `UUIDv5(BRAIN_PROXY_NAMESPACE, system_prompt + "\x00" + first_user_message)` from LLM request content. This UUID is used directly as the conversation record ID (`conversation:<uuidv5>`). Same conversation content always produces the same ID -- no lookup needed.
    18→
    19→The null byte separator (`\x00`) prevents collisions between different system_prompt/first_user_message combinations that would otherwise concatenate to the same string.
    20→
    21→UUIDv5 (deterministic, namespace-based) will not collide with UUIDv4 (random) used by the web UI, so proxy-created and UI-created conversations coexist safely in the same table.
    22→
    23→The proxy uses only existing `conversation` table fields (`workspace`, `title`, `source`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`) -- no schema changes required. The `source` field is set to `"proxy"` to distinguish from UI-created conversations.
    24→
    25→## Alternatives Considered
    26→
    27→### Alternative 1: SHA-256 Hex as Conversation Record ID
    28→
    29→Use `SHA-256(system_prompt + "\x00" + first_user_message)` truncated or full as the conversation record ID.
    30→
    31→**Rejected because**: Inconsistent with codebase conventions. All existing IDs are UUIDs (UUIDv4 for entities, RecordId-wrapped). A hex string ID would be a different format from every other conversation record. Logging, filtering, and tooling expect UUID-shaped identifiers.
    32→
    33→### Alternative 2: Random UUID + Content Hash Lookup Field
    34→
    35→Generate a random UUIDv4 for each conversation and store a `content_hash` field for deduplication. On each request, hash the content and query `SELECT id FROM conversation WHERE content_hash = $hash AND workspace = $ws`.
    36→
    37→**Rejected because**: Requires a DB lookup on every proxied request to check for existing conversations. Adds a `content_hash` field to the schema. The deterministic UUIDv5 approach eliminates both the lookup and the extra field -- the ID itself encodes the content identity.
    38→
    39→### Alternative 3: No Conversation Correlation
    40→
    41→Unknown clients get no trace grouping. Traces are linked to workspace only.
    42→
    43→**Rejected because**: Observer cannot analyze traces as a conversation unit. Cost attribution is per-trace only, not per-conversation. Session-end analysis (ADR-048) loses context for unknown clients.
    44→
    45→## Consequences
    46→
    47→### Positive
    48→
    49→- All clients get conversation grouping for free -- no integration required, no session needed
    50→- Zero DB lookup for correlation -- the deterministic ID is computed from request content
    51→- Consistent UUID format across all conversation records (proxy UUIDv5, UI UUIDv4)
    52→- No schema changes -- uses existing `conversation` table fields
    53→- Idempotent creation -- `CREATE conversation:<uuidv5> SET ...` is a no-op if the record already exists (SurrealDB CREATE returns error on existing ID; the upserter catches the error and returns the existing record)
    54→
    55→### Negative
    56→
    57→- UUIDv5 computation on every request (negligible: ~0.01ms for hash + UUID formatting)
    58→- Conversation cannot span system prompt changes -- a new system prompt produces a new conversation ID
    59→- First user message must be extractable from the request -- if the request has only system messages or is missing a system prompt, no conversation hash is computed; trace is created without conversation field populated
    60→- Conversation upsert failure does not block request forwarding -- a warning observation is logged and the trace proceeds without conversation link
    61→
    62→## References
    63→
    64→- ADR-047: Per-Trace Contradiction + Missing Decision Detection via Observer Extension
    65→- ADR-048: Observer Session-End Trace Analysis
    66→- ADR-049: Proxy Session ID Resolution -- Read-Only, No Lifecycle Management
    67→- Data models: `docs/feature/llm-proxy/design/data-models.md` Section 2
    68→- RFC 4122 Section 4.3: UUIDv5 (SHA-1 namespace-based)
    69→
```

     1→# ADR-049: Proxy Session ID Resolution -- Read-Only, No Lifecycle Management
     2→
     3→## Status
     4→
     5→Proposed
     6→
     7→## Context
     8→
     9→The LLM proxy intercepts API traffic from external coding agents (Claude Code, custom agents via Vercel AI SDK, etc.). Post-session analysis -- Observer trace scanning for missing decisions and contradictions (ADR-048) -- requires traces to be linked to agent sessions.
    10→
    11→Session lifecycle management already exists at the correct layers:
    12→- **CLI (`brain init` hooks)**: Installs SessionStart/SessionEnd hooks for Claude Code. These call `createAgentSession()` and `endAgentSession()` respectively.
    13→- **Orchestrator**: Manages sessions for Brain-managed agents via the same `createAgentSession()`/`endAgentSession()` API.
    14→
    15→The proxy sees every LLM request and can extract session identifiers from request metadata/headers. The question is whether the proxy should also manage session lifecycle (create, update activity, end via timeout) or simply read session IDs and link traces to existing sessions.
    16→
    17→See: `docs/research/llm-session-boundary-detection.md`
    18→
    19→### Constraints
    20→
    21→- Session lifecycle is already managed by CLI hooks and orchestrator
    22→- The proxy's primary obligation is transparent passthrough
    23→- Observer trace analysis (ADR-048) depends on a session-end trigger, which the existing SurrealDB EVENT on `ended_at` already provides
    24→- Adding duplicate lifecycle management creates race conditions (double-end, activity tracking conflicts)
    25→
    26→## Decision
    27→
    28→The proxy **reads session IDs only**. It never creates, updates, or ends sessions.
    29→
    30→### Session ID Extraction
    31→
    32→The proxy extracts a session ID from incoming requests via two sources:
    33→
    34→| Source | Extraction | Example |
    35→|--------|-----------|---------|
    36→| Claude Code | `metadata.user_id` field in request body | `session_abc-123-def` |
    37→| Brain-managed agents | `X-Brain-Session` header | `agent_session:7f3a...` |
    38→| Unknown client | No extraction possible | Trace linked to workspace only |
    39→
    40→This is a pure function with no DB calls or side effects. The extracted session ID is passed to the trace writer, which looks up the corresponding `agent_session` record via `external_session_id` to link the trace.
    41→
    42→### What the proxy does NOT do
    43→
    44→- Create `agent_session` records
    45→- Update `last_activity_at` or any session fields
    46→- End sessions (no inactivity timeout, no background sweep, no `setInterval`)
    47→- Track session activity
    48→- Run background timers
    49→
    50→### Session-end trigger for Observer
    51→
    52→The SurrealDB EVENT on `agent_session.ended_at` fires whenever `ended_at` transitions from NONE to a value -- regardless of who sets it (CLI, orchestrator, or any future mechanism). This EVENT triggers the Observer trace analysis pipeline (ADR-048). The proxy is not involved in this flow.
    53→
    54→## Alternatives Considered
    55→
    56→### Alternative 1: Proxy Manages Sessions with Inactivity Timeout
    57→
    58→The proxy upserts `agent_session` records on every request, tracks `last_activity_at`, and runs a `setInterval` background sweep to end sessions after N minutes of inactivity.
    59→
    60→**Rejected because**: Duplicates responsibility. The CLI already manages session lifecycle for Claude Code via `brain init` hooks. The orchestrator manages sessions for Brain-managed agents. Adding a third session manager in the proxy creates:
    61→- Double-end races (CLI ends session, then sweep also tries to end it)
    62→- Activity tracking conflicts (`last_activity_at` updated by proxy, but session state managed by CLI/orchestrator)
    63→- Unnecessary DB writes on every proxied request (upsert `last_activity_at`)
    64→- A background timer (`setInterval`) that adds operational complexity for no unique value
    65→
    66→### Alternative 2: Proxy Upserts Sessions on Each Request
    67→
    68→The proxy creates a session if none exists, but does not manage lifecycle (no timeout, no sweep).
    69→
    70→**Rejected because**: Still performs unnecessary DB writes on every LLM call. If a session already exists (created by CLI/orchestrator), the upsert is wasted work. If no session exists, the proxy should not be the one creating it -- that is the CLI/orchestrator's responsibility.
    71→
    72→## Consequences
    73→
    74→### Positive
    75→
    76→- Zero DB writes from the proxy for session management (read-only lookup)
    77→- No background timers or sweep logic in the proxy
    78→- No race conditions with CLI/orchestrator session lifecycle
    79→- Simpler proxy code -- session ID resolution is a pure function
    80→- Single source of truth for session lifecycle (CLI/orchestrator)
    81→
    82→### Negative
    83→
    84→- Agents not managed by CLI or orchestrator will have no session -- their traces are linked to workspace only
    85→- No automatic session creation for unknown clients (requires explicit `brain init` or orchestrator integration)
    86→
    87→### Mitigations
    88→
    89→- Unknown-client traces are still captured and linked to workspace, enabling workspace-level analysis
    90→- As Brain adoption grows, more agents will use `brain init` or orchestrator integration
    91→
    92→## References
    93→
    94→- ADR-040: LLM Proxy In-Process Module
    95→- ADR-048: Observer Session-End Trace Analysis
    96→- Research: `docs/research/llm-session-boundary-detection.md`
    97→
```

Now I have the context. ADR-049 says proxy is read-only for sessions, and ADR-050 uses conversations for hash correlation. This PR supersedes both — the proxy now upserts `agent_session` records (not conversations) using the content hash as fallback session ID.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/marcus/conductor/workspaces/brain-v1/chicago/docs/adrs/ADR-052-proxy-session-not-conversation.md",
  "content": "# ADR-052: Proxy Upserts Agent Sessions, Not Conversations\n\n## Status\n\nProposed — supersedes ADR-049 and ADR-050\n\n## Context\n\nThe LLM proxy was correlating traces into `conversation` records using a UUIDv5 content hash (ADR-050). However, `conversation` is a chat UI entity — it models the back-and-forth between a human and Brain's chat agent. Proxy traffic comes from external coding agents (Claude Code, Cursor, custom agents), which are not chat UI conversations. Mixing the two domains in the same table conflates semantics and creates confusion for Observer analysis, feed rendering, and cost attribution.\n\nMeanwhile, ADR-049 established the proxy as read-only for sessions — it would never create or update `agent_session` records, deferring lifecycle to CLI hooks and orchestrators. This was sound when all agents used `brain init`, but unknown clients (no session header, no metadata) produced orphaned traces linked only to a workspace. Observer session-end analysis (ADR-048) cannot run on traces that have no session.\n\nThe `agent_session` table is the correct grouping entity for agent work. The content hash mechanism from ADR-050 is still valuable — but it should produce session IDs, not conversation IDs.\n\n## Decision\n\nThe proxy upserts `agent_session` records instead of `conversation` records. The content hash (UUIDv5 of system prompt + first user message) serves as a **fallback** session ID when no explicit session signal is available.\n\n### Session ID resolution priority\n\n| Priority | Source | Signal |\n|----------|--------|--------|\n| 1 | `X-Brain-Session` header | Explicit session ID from orchestrator |\n| 2 | `metadata.user_id` (Claude Code) | Session extracted from `user_<hash>_account_<uuid>_session_<uuid>` |\n| 3 | Content hash (UUIDv5) | Deterministic from system prompt + first user message |\n\nWhen priority 1 or 2 resolves a session, the proxy links the trace to the existing `agent_session` via `resolveAgentSessionId` (DB lookup by `external_session_id`). When only priority 3 is available, the proxy **upserts** an `agent_session` record using the content hash as the session ID.\n\n### Agent identity on hash-derived sessions\n\nHash-derived sessions have no CLI/orchestrator to set the `agent` field. The proxy resolves the agent name from available signals:\n\n| Priority | Source | Value |\n|----------|--------|-------|\n| 1 | `X-Brain-Agent-Type` header | Explicit agent type (e.g. `\"coding-agent\"`, `\"architect\"`) |\n| 2 | `User-Agent` header containing `\"claude-cli\"` | `\"claude-cli\"` |\n| 3 | Fallback | `\"proxy\"` |\n\n### Schema change\n\nA `source` field is added to `agent_session`:\n\n```sql\nDEFINE FIELD OVERWRITE source ON agent_session TYPE option<string>;\n```\n\nThe proxy sets `source: \"proxy\"` on upserted sessions to distinguish them from CLI/orchestrator-created sessions. This enables Observer and UI to filter or annotate proxy-inferred sessions differently.\n\n### What changed from ADR-049\n\nADR-049 prohibited the proxy from creating sessions. This ADR relaxes that constraint **only for the content-hash fallback path**. When an explicit session ID is present (priority 1 or 2), the proxy remains read-only — it looks up the existing session and links the trace. The proxy still does not manage session lifecycle (no `ended_at`, no inactivity timeout, no background sweep).\n\n### What changed from ADR-050\n\nThe `conversation-upserter` is removed. The content hash resolver is renamed from `conversation-hash-resolver` to `session-hash-resolver`. Its output type changes from `ConversationHash` to `SessionHash`. The UUIDv5 namespace constant is renamed to `BRAIN_PROXY_SESSION_NS`. The deterministic ID mechanism is identical — only the target table changes from `conversation` to `agent_session`.\n\n## Alternatives Considered\n\n### Alternative 1: Keep Conversations for Proxy, Add Session Link Later\n\nContinue upserting `conversation` records from the proxy and add an `agent_session` link as a separate field on the conversation.\n\n**Rejected because**: A conversation is a chat UI concept with its own lifecycle (messages, streaming, onboarding state). Proxy traces are not conversations — they are agent work sessions. Adding a session link to a conversation record does not fix the semantic mismatch; it papers over it with an extra indirection.\n\n### Alternative 2: Create a New `proxy_session` Table\n\nIntroduce a dedicated table for proxy-inferred sessions, separate from `agent_session`.\n\n**Rejected because**: Proxy-inferred sessions and CLI/orchestrator sessions serve the same purpose — grouping agent traces for analysis. A separate table would require Observer, feed, and cost attribution to query two tables. The `source` field on `agent_session` provides sufficient distinction without table proliferation.\n\n### Alternative 3: No Fallback — Unknown Clients Get No Session\n\nOnly create session links when explicit session signals (priority 1 or 2) are present. Unknown clients produce workspace-only traces.\n\n**Rejected because**: This was the ADR-049 approach. In practice, many agents do not send session headers. Observer session-end analysis (ADR-048) cannot run on sessionless traces. The content hash fallback is low-cost (one upsert per unique system prompt + first message) and provides grouping for all clients.\n\n## Consequences\n\n### Positive\n\n- Correct domain modeling — agent work is tracked in `agent_session`, chat UI stays in `conversation`\n- All proxy clients get session grouping — no integration required\n- Observer session-end analysis works for all clients, not just those with `brain init`\n- `source: \"proxy\"` enables filtering proxy-inferred sessions from CLI-managed ones\n- Content hash mechanism preserved — zero-lookup correlation still works, just targets the right table\n\n### Negative\n\n- Proxy now writes to `agent_session` on the fallback path (one upsert per unique content hash, not per request)\n- Hash-derived sessions have no `ended_at` — Observer session-end trigger does not fire for them (same as before with conversations; a future inactivity timeout ADR can address this)\n- Agent identity on hash-derived sessions is best-effort — `User-Agent` detection is heuristic-based\n\n## References\n\n- ADR-049: Proxy Session ID Resolution (superseded — proxy was read-only)\n- ADR-050: Conversation Hash Correlation via UUIDv5 (superseded — used conversation table)\n- ADR-048: Observer Session-End Trace Analysis\n- ADR-040: LLM Proxy In-Process Module\n- Migration: `schema/migrations/0049_agent_session_source_field.surql`\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Created `docs/adrs/ADR-052-proxy-session-not-conversation.md` and marked ADR-049 and ADR-050 as superseded.

Key points in the ADR:
- **Problem**: `conversation` is a chat UI entity; proxy was creating conversations for agent work
- **Decision**: Proxy upserts `agent_session` instead, with content hash as fallback session ID
- **Agent identity**: resolved from `X-Brain-Agent-Type` > `User-Agent` containing `claude-cli` > `"proxy"` fallback
- **`source: "proxy"`** field distinguishes proxy-inferred sessions from CLI/orchestrator ones
- Supersedes ADR-049 (proxy read-only) and ADR-050 (conversation hash)

```
     1→/**
     2→ * Session Upserter — Idempotent DB Adapter
     3→ *
     4→ * Creates or updates an agent_session record in SurrealDB using native UPSERT
     5→ * with the deterministic UUIDv5 session ID from the hash resolver.
     6→ *
     7→ * This replaces the conversation upserter — the proxy groups traces via
     8→ * agent_session (invoked edges), not conversation records.
     9→ *
    10→ * Port: (SessionUpsertInput, Dependencies) -> Promise<string | undefined>
    11→ * Side effects: SurrealDB writes (boundary adapter)
    12→ */
    13→
    14→import { RecordId } from "surrealdb";
    15→import type { Surreal } from "surrealdb";
    16→import { logInfo, logError } from "../http/observability";
    17→
    18→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→// Types
    20→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    21→
    22→export type SessionUpsertInput = {
    23→  readonly sessionId: string;
    24→  readonly workspaceId: string;
    25→  readonly title: string;
    26→  readonly agent: string;
    27→};
    28→
    29→type SessionUpsertDependencies = {
    30→  readonly surreal: Surreal;
    31→};
    32→
    33→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    34→// Public API
    35→// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    36→
    37→/**
    38→ * Upsert an agent_session record from a content-derived hash.
    39→ * Returns the session PK (string) on success, undefined on failure.
    40→ * Failure never throws -- it logs a warning and returns undefined
    41→ * so the caller can proceed without blocking.
    42→ */
    43→export async function upsertProxySession(
    44→  input: SessionUpsertInput,
    45→  deps: SessionUpsertDependencies,
    46→): Promise<string | undefined> {
    47→  const sessionRecord = new RecordId("agent_session", input.sessionId);
    48→  const workspaceRecord = new RecordId("workspace", input.workspaceId);
    49→
    50→  try {
    51→    const results = await deps.surreal.query<[Array<{ id: RecordId }>]>(
    52→      `UPSERT $sess MERGE {
    53→        agent: $agent,
    54→        workspace: $workspace,
    55→        source: "proxy_hash",
    56→        started_at: started_at ?? time::now(),
    57→        last_event_at: time::now(),
    58→        created_at: created_at ?? time::now()
    59→      };`,
    60→      {
    61→        sess: sessionRecord,
    62→        workspace: workspaceRecord,
    63→        agent: input.agent,
    64→      },
    65→    );
    66→
    67→    const record = results[0]?.[0];
    68→    if (record) {
    69→      logInfo("proxy.session.upserted", "Proxy session upserted via content hash", {
    70→        session_id: input.sessionId,
    71→        workspace_id: input.workspaceId,
    72→      });
    73→      return record.id.id as string;
    74→    }
    75→  } catch (error) {
    76→    logError(
    77→      "proxy.session.upsert_failed",
    78→      "Failed to upsert proxy session record",
    79→      error,
    80→    );
    81→  }
    82→
    83→  return undefined;
    84→}
    85→
```
